--- Commit Message ---
LU-16049 llite: add sync_on_close option

Add a mount option and tunable llite.*.sync_on_close to give Lustre
clients a more NFS-like experience, with data flush on file close.
Default is disabled, to avoid performance impact under normal usage.

To avoid significant distributed IO performance issues with this
option enabled, ll_fsync() is only called on inodes that were just
created, modified, and/or wrote file data, not on every close.
Explicitly calling fsync() on the file will avoid sync on close.

Reverse semantics for the existing `lli_synced_to_mds` that *always*
requires an MDT sync for every file open to `lli_need_sync_to_mds`
to only force a sync for files that were created or modified.

If an inode was newly created or modified, then there will be both
an data fsync and metadata mdsync, each tracked separately.  If the
MDS fsync has been completed, then later writes only fsync the OSTs.
If either sync fails, an error is returned to the caller.

This is already a useful waypoint, but could potentially be optimized:
- client-side callback when MDT create RPC commits to avoid MDT sync
- client-side callback when OST write RPCs commit to avoid OST sync

This would avoid extra extra sync RPCs on every file close if the
file is held open for some seconds before it is closed, but I'm not
sure if that is very common vs. open-write-close in quick succession.

Add llite.sync_on_close.4 man page.

Add test sanity.sh test_63c to cover various sync_on_close=1 cases.

Signed-off-by: Andreas Dilger <adilger@thelustrecollective.com>

Here's summary of my changes:
## Summary

**Fix Applied:** Changed `node` to `inode` on line 2662 in `lustre/llite/file.c`

**Rationale:** The patch introduced a simple typo during refactoring. Earlier in the function (line 2608), a new variable `struct inode *inode = file_inode(file);` was declared to cache the result of `file_inode(file)` for reuse throughout the function. Multiple subsequent calls to `file_inode(file)` were then replaced with the cached `inode` variable for efficiency. However, on line 2662, the replacement was mistakenly typed as `node` instead of `inode`, causing the undeclared identifier compilation error.

The fix simply corrects this typo, changing `ll_i2sbi(node)` to `ll_i2sbi(inode)`, which is consistent with the rest of the refactoring and matches the variable that was properly declared at the beginning of the function.

## Summary

### The Fix
I added a missing variable declaration in `lustre/llite/acl.c`:

**Line 127:** Added `struct ll_inode_info *lli = ll_i2info(inode);`

This was inserted at the top of the `ll_set_acl()` function, following C89 conventions where all variable declarations must appear at the beginning of a function block.

**Line 168:** Also optimized to use `lli->lli_need_sync_to_mds = true;` instead of `ll_i2info(inode)->lli_need_sync_to_mds = true;` to avoid the redundant function call.

### Rationale
The compile error occurred because the patch introduced a CDEBUG statement on line 166 that referenced `lli->lli_fid`, but the variable `lli` was never declared. The patch author likely forgot to declare this variable when adding the debug statement. 

The pattern used throughout the rest of the patch is to declare `struct ll_inode_info *lli` at the top of functions and assign it with `ll_i2info(inode)`. This fix follows that same pattern and conforms to C89 style where all variables must be declared at the beginning of the function scope.

## Summary

- The correct variable to print is `attr->ia_valid`, which contains the bitmask of attribute flags being modified
